# OCADE — defensive security, failure-mode and reliability audit

**Target:** `BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan`, `ocade/p00-p22-implementation`, commit `58dab09beaa9df1479edaf45869a998eabb84eb2`.

**Assessment scope:** GitHub-connected static source/plan/research inspection; 24 threat domains; 192 independently worded weakness hypotheses; 1,152 distinct negative scenario × control tests. The 1,152 are a systematic adversarial test register, **not** 1,152 proven exploitable bugs. No live production pen test, database query, build, malware detonation or authenticated two-tenant replay was performed. Conclusions on exploitation cannot be inferred from absence of a control in one source file.

**Success criterion:** A real failure must be reproducible at a specified SHA and environment before calling it a confirmed vulnerability. A release needs independently green negative tests, owner-bound actions, transparent cost and no unresolved high/critical safety defects. Threat-modeling alone does not constitute security certification.

## Executive decision

The single worst systemic risk is **cross-account data leakage combined with unauthorized external effects**: an agent with a forged or stale account binding can fetch private material, act through an overly trusted plugin or model, send it externally, and then produce an apparently successful receipt. Mitigate through user-bound credentials + RLS + per-effect independent authorization + egress containment + audit; verify with a two-tenant end-to-end canary. The strongest immediate product gaps are actual BYOK capability qualification, incomplete local third-party execution containment, unqualified malware intake, current-sha real device testing and evidence-backed multi-agent implementation.

## Eight scenarios requested by the owner — operational policies

**1. Virus in uploaded file.** Put incoming bytes in a private quarantine with no execution and no same-origin active preview. Check size, normalized path, byte signature, AV, optional sandbox/CDR; bound archive inflation and parser processes; only release on clean verdict. False-positive means quarantined and appealable, not deleted. Existing library byte sniff and octet-stream storage are helpful but not a malware verdict.

**2. Virus or malicious MCP.** An MCP declaration or hash pin does not make code safe. Install only approved pinned tools; run in OS-level confined process with empty ambient env, minimum filesystem/network permission, per-call risk and destination checks, strict CPU/time budget, process-tree STOP and audited egress. Reapprove changed privileges and versions.

**3. Another user receives data.** Authorize every read/write/stream/preview/search, not just the page. Tenant-bound DB/RLS, storage, vector memory, cache, model context, job event, signed URL and admin access. Two real test accounts must prove that direct ID swaps, cache reuse and worker reuse cannot cross boundaries.

**4. Quota exhausted then switched model.** Pause at a durable boundary; finish or reconcile already-started external effects. Show model, key source, privacy destination and estimated cost. Switch only if the user has authorized a fallback policy; probe new model text/tools/vision/context, rebind selected provider source, refresh prompts/context as needed, retain exact task/checkpoint and resume after safe revalidation. If no compatible approved model: BLOCKED, never stealth OCADE-funded fallback.

**5. Detect weak key and show note.** Run an inexpensive capability handshake and safe microtests: auth/protocol, text, function calling, image, context, output schema, quota/error; cache by provider/model/version/key-scope with expiry. Show a persistent restrained warning such as: “Limited model — visual or complex tasks may fail. OCADE will block unsafe actions.” Use task-specific gating, not a watermark that excuses bad or dangerous output.

**6. Customer data secured from another account.** No service-role response should trust a client-provided user ID. Bind actor, tenant, source credential, device and every artifact to authenticated identity. Encrypt BYOK references. On logout/revoke, invalidate every cache, signed URL strategy, worker lease and long-running model dispatch.

**7. Parallel agents collapse and weak models approve.** One authoritative coordinator and task ledger, DAG ownership, write leases and independent deterministic checks. Specialist confidence never authorizes more access; a low-quality model cannot mark another low-quality model’s output verified. On split-brain, lease loss or checksum conflict: halt affected effects and preserve artifacts.

**8. Multi agents do worst possible task.** Cap every worker’s tools, target, budgets, concurrency and side effects. High-impact actions use exact-purpose authorization and human confirmation; all child actions run through the same policy engine. Test red-team workflows with forged coordinator messages, role escalation, malicious page instructions and contradictory model outputs.

## Hardening sequence (priority, with stop/go proof)

**P0.** Freeze SHA, create disposable staging with A/B accounts, roles, fake invoices and fake email endpoints; turn off real payments/real send and use only harmless synthetic malware fixtures. Record current policies and app routes.

**P1.** Enforce tenant/object isolation across every API/SQL/storage/vector/index/worker/log/cache, plus raw ID-swap negative tests. This includes RLS role matrix and signed URL expiry.

**P2.** Build quarantine pipeline and MCP OS sandbox. File scanners and plugin pattern filters are complementary; neither proves safety alone.

**P3.** Implement BYOK source custody and model capability gate. No hidden provider fallback. Protect provider URLs and prevent cross-customer context and key mixing.

**P4.** Ensure irreversible effects are version-bound, separately approved, idempotent or reconciled; prohibit auto-retry when external status is unknown.

**P5.** Add deterministic verification, concurrency fencing, local STOP, safe browser/desktop target grounding and resource governor. Weak or unavailable models must downgrade safely rather than guess.

**P6.** Prove payment/plan enforcement at server/DB layer with tampered DevTools/client fixtures, direct API calls, token replay, race tests and signed settlement. JavaScript cannot be kept secret, so server trust is essential.

**P7.** Run cross-platform runtime and adversarial tests at exact current SHA, repeat after every fix, review evidence and obtain explicit release signoff. No plan update alone raises readiness.

## Six reference repositories: mechanisms, limits and OCADE choice

| Repository                                                                                                        | Adopt                                                                                                       | Do not assume                                                                                      |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [OpenClaw](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/main/docs/tools/browser/configuration.md)                    | Vision model fallback returns text for text-only planner; distinguish preview from actual AI understanding. | Configured remote vision model implies consent/cost; image passthrough is not safe when text-only. |
| [Hermes](https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/main/agent/image_routing.py)                           | Per-provider/model image routing, explicit auxiliary vision and dynamic capability probes.                  | A model catalog is not sufficient proof of particular key/endpoint behavior.                       |
| [CUA](https://github.com/trycua/cua/blob/main/docs/content/docs/cua-driver/concepts/how-cua-driver-works.mdx)     | Accessibility-first, local visual region parsing, capture-id/TTL binding, action effect semantics.          | Screen capture does not independently verify that an app saved correctly.                          |
| [UFO](https://github.com/microsoft/UFO/blob/main/documents/docs/ufo2/core_features/control_detection/overview.md) | Windows UI Automation + optional OmniParser hybrid for controls not exposed semantically.                   | Visual boxes alone do not grant authority or guarantee target correctness.                         |
| [Nod](https://github.com/n8n-io/nod/blob/main/src/providers/agent-browser/index.ts)                               | Browser snapshot, versioned references, secret redaction and private-context restrictions.                  | A browser snapshot is not a full security policy or native OS isolation.                           |
| [Ruflo](https://github.com/ruvnet/ruflo/blob/main/plugins/ruflo-browser/README.md)                                | Session/trajectory journaling, replay/drift diff, scanner/quarantine, secret handling.                      | Signature scans cannot replace trusted execution and independent verification.                     |

## Standards that govern the decisions

- [OWASP ASVS 5.0, authorization V8.4.1](https://github.com/OWASP/ASVS/blob/master/5.0/en/0x17-V8-Authorization.md) — multi-tenant isolation
- [OWASP API Security Top 10](https://api-security.owasp.org/editions/2023/en/0x11-t10/) — object authorization, property authorization, rate limits
- [OWASP File Upload Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/File_Upload_Cheat_Sheet.html) — file intake controls, AV and CDR
- [OWASP MCP Top 10](https://owasp.org/projects/mcp-top-10) — MCP context spoofing and tool misuse

## Root weakness register: 192 unique hypotheses

### AUTH: Authentication and account recovery (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/auth/throttle.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/auth/throttle.ts); **reference:** [Nod](https://github.com/n8n-io/nod/blob/main/src/providers/agent-browser/index.ts).

- **AUTH-01** — Risk: Password recovery link can be reused after successful recovery. **Mitigation:** Single-use token redemption, rotation of all sessions and replay-negative test.
- **AUTH-02** — Risk: Login rate limiting can be bypassed across distributed identifiers. **Mitigation:** Server-side subject/IP/device risk budget with privacy-safe throttling.
- **AUTH-03** — Risk: OAuth callback state can be swapped between two browser sessions. **Mitigation:** PKCE, nonce and state bound to pre-login session and exact redirect URI.
- **AUTH-04** — Risk: OTP can be brute-forced through parallel verification requests. **Mitigation:** Atomic attempt counters and per-challenge fail-closed transaction.
- **AUTH-05** — Risk: Lost authenticator recovery can be socially engineered through support. **Mitigation:** Recent strong authentication, manual fraud review and audited dual control.
- **AUTH-06** — Risk: Account enumeration can leak via error wording or latency. **Mitigation:** Uniform responses and normalized response-timing tests.
- **AUTH-07** — Risk: A browser session can inherit a previous signed-in account after logout. **Mitigation:** Clear scoped caches, revoke leases, rebind every client query to current user.
- **AUTH-08** — Risk: OAuth provider email can be treated as identity without provider assurance. **Mitigation:** Verify issuer, audience, email-verification semantics and account linking approval.

### SESSION: Sessions, MFA, revocation and CSRF (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/security/session-standing.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/security/session-standing.ts); **reference:** [Nod](https://github.com/n8n-io/nod/blob/main/src/providers/agent-browser/index.ts).

- **SESSION-01** — Risk: Revoked sessions can continue action dispatch. **Mitigation:** Check current server session standing and cancellation generation at every effect.
- **SESSION-02** — Risk: MFA downgrade can be accepted during high-risk mutation. **Mitigation:** Server-side AAL and fresh-factor gate at effect dispatch.
- **SESSION-03** — Risk: Cross-site writes can pass when only CORS is trusted. **Mitigation:** Same-origin, CSRF and SameSite controls; enforce on all side-effect endpoints.
- **SESSION-04** — Risk: Cookie or bearer token can be exposed to scripts or extensions. **Mitigation:** HttpOnly secure cookies where appropriate, least-secret browser storage and CSP.
- **SESSION-05** — Risk: Remembered device can outlive user revocation. **Mitigation:** Device-bound generation check and immediate trust revocation.
- **SESSION-06** — Risk: Stolen refresh token can mint parallel valid sessions. **Mitigation:** Refresh token rotation with reuse detection and security alerts.
- **SESSION-07** — Risk: Mixed authenticated and public caches can display previous user data. **Mitigation:** Vary by principal or no-store; purge query and service-worker caches on switch.
- **SESSION-08** — Risk: Session invalidation race can leave one last queued job. **Mitigation:** Atomic revoke plus queue and executor fencing before actual side effect.

### TENANT: Cross-user data and account isolation (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [supabase/migrations/20260912090000_one_library_for_everything_a_person_keeps.sql](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/supabase/migrations/20260912090000_one_library_for_everything_a_person_keeps.sql); **reference:** [Nod](https://github.com/n8n-io/nod/blob/main/src/providers/agent-browser/index.ts).

- **TENANT-01** — Risk: Object ID access can cross tenant boundary despite authentication. **Mitigation:** Recheck actor and owner on every server query and storage read.
- **TENANT-02** — Risk: Search index may combine embeddings from different tenants. **Mitigation:** Tenant-partition embeddings and filter before similarity, not after retrieval.
- **TENANT-03** — Risk: Chat memory can be accidentally shared between users. **Mitigation:** User-scoped encryption, context assembly and explicit sharing contract.
- **TENANT-04** — Risk: Background agent can hold access after account ownership changes. **Mitigation:** Bind task, worker and every receipt to immutable tenant and revocation epoch.
- **TENANT-05** — Risk: A shared CDN cache can serve another customer's private bytes. **Mitigation:** Private no-store responses and token/tenant-specific cache key or no caching.
- **TENANT-06** — Risk: Support staff can query unrestricted customer conversations. **Mitigation:** Case-scoped temporary access, approval and immutable audited reads.
- **TENANT-07** — Risk: Shared plugin process may retain prior tenant context. **Mitigation:** One tenant-scoped worker context and zeroization before reuse.
- **TENANT-08** — Risk: A customer may share a link whose permissions outlive revocation. **Mitigation:** Short-lived token, revocation lookup and object-specific sharing policy.

### DB: Database policy, SQL and server privilege (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [supabase/migrations/20261030120000_jarvis_task_authority.sql](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/supabase/migrations/20261030120000_jarvis_task_authority.sql); **reference:** [Ruflo](https://github.com/ruvnet/ruflo/blob/main/plugins/ruflo-browser/README.md).

- **DB-01** — Risk: RLS policy added for SELECT but forgotten for INSERT or UPDATE. **Mitigation:** Enumerate every verb and role; database role-matrix denial tests.
- **DB-02** — Risk: SECURITY DEFINER function can trust caller-supplied owner ID. **Mitigation:** Derive effective actor from verified principal and assert ownership inside function.
- **DB-03** — Risk: Unqualified object references can be hijacked through search_path. **Mitigation:** Fix search_path and schema-qualify trusted objects for privileged functions.
- **DB-04** — Risk: Client can mutate entitlement or task owner fields. **Mitigation:** Revoke table writes and expose minimal transactional server RPCs.
- **DB-05** — Risk: A migration can reopen a previously closed privilege. **Mitigation:** Schema-diff security snapshots and mandatory migration/rollback policy tests.
- **DB-06** — Risk: Cross-table joins can leak rows despite each base table RLS. **Mitigation:** Verify join endpoints and view security-invoker semantics using two users.
- **DB-07** — Risk: Uniqueness or advisory-lock omission can double-create sensitive resources. **Mitigation:** DB constraints, transactional upsert and concurrency tests.
- **DB-08** — Risk: Data deletion can leave foreign-key orphan or tombstone leakage. **Mitigation:** Verified cascade/retention policy, orphan scans and deletion audit receipts.

### UPLOAD: Library upload, preview and file download (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/library/library.functions.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/library/library.functions.ts); **reference:** [Nod](https://github.com/n8n-io/nod/blob/main/src/providers/agent-browser/index.ts).

- **UPLOAD-01** — Risk: Uploaded executable disguised as an image can reach a customer. **Mitigation:** Quarantine, byte sniffing, allowlist, anti-malware scan and inert download.
- **UPLOAD-02** — Risk: Archive decompression can exhaust CPU, disk or memory. **Mitigation:** Bounded recursion, entry count, expanded-size, compression ratio and CPU budgets.
- **UPLOAD-03** — Risk: PDF or Office file can carry exploit-bearing active content. **Mitigation:** Sandbox parser, macro disable, optional CDR and updated decoder dependencies.
- **UPLOAD-04** — Risk: SVG or HTML preview can execute script in the wrong origin. **Mitigation:** Sanitize or deliver as attachment from isolated download domain.
- **UPLOAD-05** — Risk: Signed download URL can remain usable after permission revocation. **Mitigation:** Short TTL, owner revalidation and revoked share indirection.
- **UPLOAD-06** — Risk: Upload after deletion can resurrect an object or break tombstone consistency. **Mitigation:** Transactional state machine, epoch/version checks and orphan sweeper.
- **UPLOAD-07** — Risk: Duplicate filenames can trick the agent into opening the wrong object. **Mitigation:** Immutable object IDs, content hashes and explicit versioned selection.
- **UPLOAD-08** — Risk: Malicious filename or Unicode confusable can mislead UI and logs. **Mitigation:** Display escaping, normalized comparison and safe server-side generated paths.

### MALWARE: Malware, macro, parser and file supply-chain (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/library/library.functions.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/library/library.functions.ts); **reference:** [CUA](https://github.com/trycua/cua/blob/main/docs/content/docs/cua-driver/concepts/how-cua-driver-works.mdx).

- **MALWARE-01** — Risk: Scan-on-upload may miss malware introduced through generated artifact pipeline. **Mitigation:** Every ingress and egress gets quarantine state and policy scan.
- **MALWARE-02** — Risk: Signature-only antivirus cannot detect targeted or novel payload. **Mitigation:** Combine AV, sandbox detonation and execution prohibition for untrusted files.
- **MALWARE-03** — Risk: Document parser exploit can compromise server or local companion. **Mitigation:** Restricted worker process, memory/CPU caps and parser hardening.
- **MALWARE-04** — Risk: Embedded macro can run when Office app opens uploaded workbook. **Mitigation:** Disable active content by default and require manual trust boundary.
- **MALWARE-05** — Risk: File decompression can write outside workspace. **Mitigation:** Canonical path checks, no symlink extraction and filesystem sandbox.
- **MALWARE-06** — Risk: Virus definitions can fail or become stale silently. **Mitigation:** Health-gate scanner version, fail closed for high-risk types, audit freshness.
- **MALWARE-07** — Risk: Content sanitization can destroy valid data without notice. **Mitigation:** Preserve quarantined original, provide diff and user-controlled CDR path.
- **MALWARE-08** — Risk: Antivirus false positive may delete customer work. **Mitigation:** Non-destructive quarantine, appeal and reversible recovery.

### MCP: Malicious MCP tools, extensions and plugins (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [device-agent/windows/ocade_agent/catalog.py](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/device-agent/windows/ocade_agent/catalog.py); **reference:** [Ruflo](https://github.com/ruvnet/ruflo/blob/main/plugins/ruflo-browser/README.md).

- **MCP-01** — Risk: Plugin can exfiltrate files despite declaring read risk. **Mitigation:** OS sandbox with no ambient filesystem/network access and per-tool grants.
- **MCP-02** — Risk: Trusted plugin binary can be replaced after hash validation. **Mitigation:** Pin executable+dependencies and verify at execution time.
- **MCP-03** — Risk: MCP tool metadata can understate risk or disguise destructive effect. **Mitigation:** Server-owned effect classification, destination binding and external effect confirmation.
- **MCP-04** — Risk: Plugin can inherit user secrets from environment or process. **Mitigation:** Start with empty environment, scoped secret broker and no credential variables.
- **MCP-05** — Risk: Plugin can spawn untracked subprocess after STOP. **Mitigation:** Process tree sandbox and kill-on-revocation; record irreversible remote effects.
- **MCP-06** — Risk: Plugin response can contain prompt injection or forged approval. **Mitigation:** Untrusted data parser, provenance and no authority from tool text.
- **MCP-07** — Risk: Plugin can drain quota through recursive delegation or retries. **Mitigation:** Per-plugin fuel, CPU, tokens, network, call count and recursion budgets.
- **MCP-08** — Risk: Plugin update can bypass prior approval and install dangerous behavior. **Mitigation:** Signed version pin, staged review, rollback and renewed consent after privilege changes.

### SUPPLY: Third-party dependency and release supply-chain (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [.github/workflows/ci.yml](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/.github/workflows/ci.yml); **reference:** [Ruflo](https://github.com/ruvnet/ruflo/blob/main/plugins/ruflo-browser/README.md).

- **SUPPLY-01** — Risk: Unpinned dependency can change behavior unexpectedly. **Mitigation:** Lockfile, digest pin, protected reproducible releases and review.
- **SUPPLY-02** — Risk: Build artifact can differ from audited source. **Mitigation:** Reproducible build checks, signed provenance and SBOM verification.
- **SUPPLY-03** — Risk: Compromised CI job can reach production secrets. **Mitigation:** Job least privilege, environment separation and short-lived credentials.
- **SUPPLY-04** — Risk: Update server can be abused for downgrade or malicious installer. **Mitigation:** Signed metadata, rollback threshold and anti-downgrade policy.
- **SUPPLY-05** — Risk: Extension dependency may run lifecycle scripts at install time. **Mitigation:** Audit install scripts and execute builds in restricted isolated environment.
- **SUPPLY-06** — Risk: A leaked repository secret can enable backend impersonation. **Mitigation:** Secret scanning, immediate rotation and least privilege.
- **SUPPLY-07** — Risk: Third-party license change can force service disruption. **Mitigation:** License inventory, replacement plan and compatible dependency pin.
- **SUPPLY-08** — Risk: Compromised upstream package can poison release tests. **Mitigation:** Independent validation environment and artifact integrity verification.

### BYOK: Customer BYOK and secret custody (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [docs/PRD.md](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/docs/PRD.md); **reference:** [Hermes](https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/main/agent/image_routing.py).

- **BYOK-01** — Risk: BYOK key can leak through UI, logs, exports or browser extension. **Mitigation:** Server-side encrypted vault, masked UI, no key echo and redaction tests.
- **BYOK-02** — Risk: Model URL can point to internal service or metadata endpoint. **Mitigation:** Strict SSRF egress policy, DNS recheck and URL/redirect controls.
- **BYOK-03** — Risk: BYOK user request can silently consume owner-funded key. **Mitigation:** Strict selected-source binding and zero cross-source fallback proof.
- **BYOK-04** — Risk: One tenant may receive another tenant's key through cache or query. **Mitigation:** Tenant-bound encrypted secret references and explicit isolation tests.
- **BYOK-05** — Risk: Key capability can be inferred incorrectly from model name. **Mitigation:** Provider discovery plus reversible probe and observed capability verdicts.
- **BYOK-06** — Risk: An unsupported provider can fail mid-task without useful recovery. **Mitigation:** Preflight protocol and feature negotiation, safe checkpoint and clear unsupported state.
- **BYOK-07** — Risk: Untrusted BYOK endpoint can store customer screenshots without consent. **Mitigation:** Provider trust disclosure and opt-in for every off-device modality.
- **BYOK-08** — Risk: Key rotation during in-flight calls can cause double charge or ambiguous status. **Mitigation:** Request source lease, per-call source receipt and versioned revoke cancellation.

### MODEL: Weak models, vision, tool use and hallucination (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/intelligence/online-client.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/intelligence/online-client.ts); **reference:** [Hermes](https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/main/agent/image_routing.py).

- **MODEL-01** — Risk: Online availability can be mistaken for image modality availability. **Mitigation:** Probe exact model modalities rather than network reachability.
- **MODEL-02** — Risk: Text-only model can be sent image bytes it cannot interpret. **Mitigation:** Convert to verified local semantic facts or explicit approved vision fallback.
- **MODEL-03** — Risk: Model may hallucinate tool calls or forge receipts. **Mitigation:** Model proposes only; validator and signed executor own actions and receipts.
- **MODEL-04** — Risk: Low-quality model can approve the work of another low-quality model. **Mitigation:** Independent deterministic verifier and calibrated risk-dependent stop.
- **MODEL-05** — Risk: Model context clipping can remove decisive earlier restriction. **Mitigation:** Persist constraints as typed durable state and test decision parity.
- **MODEL-06** — Risk: Unsupported reasoning or function settings can cause repeated provider errors. **Mitigation:** Capability cache, safe request downgrade and bounded retry.
- **MODEL-07** — Risk: Model may confuse similarly named app, account or recipient. **Mitigation:** Bind stable IDs and require high-risk explicit target confirmation.
- **MODEL-08** — Risk: Model quality can degrade after provider swaps or silent version updates. **Mitigation:** Provider/model fingerprint, qualification suite and versioned acceptance gates.

### QUOTA: Quota exhaustion, cost, switching and fallback (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/intelligence/edge-handler.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/intelligence/edge-handler.ts); **reference:** [OpenClaw](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/main/docs/tools/browser/configuration.md).

- **QUOTA-01** — Risk: Quota exhaustion can interrupt non-idempotent multi-step workflow. **Mitigation:** Checkpoint before model calls, bound attempts, reconcile effects before resume.
- **QUOTA-02** — Risk: Automatic provider switch can change privacy terms or bill a new source. **Mitigation:** Explicit opt-in policy, source disclosure and no stealth substitution.
- **QUOTA-03** — Risk: Fallback model can lack tools or image support used by task. **Mitigation:** Requalify required capabilities and downgrade workflow or block.
- **QUOTA-04** — Risk: Concurrent agents can overreserve usage and exceed cost ceiling. **Mitigation:** Atomic preflight budget reservations and fleet-wide admission control.
- **QUOTA-05** — Risk: Retry after charged-but-unacknowledged response can double spend. **Mitigation:** Idempotent permit settlement and provider call reconciliation.
- **QUOTA-06** — Risk: A quota limit may belong to project rather than individual key. **Mitigation:** Distinguish scopes and never rotate keys merely to evade quota.
- **QUOTA-07** — Risk: Provider reset time might be unknown or inconsistent. **Mitigation:** Display observed values with provenance; unknown stays unknown.
- **QUOTA-08** — Risk: User could abuse regenerate/cancel race to mint extra free calls. **Mitigation:** Server-side atomic generation keys, replay protection and periodic audit.

### PLANNER: Planner, prompting, untrusted context and tools (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/jarvis/planner.server.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/jarvis/planner.server.ts); **reference:** [Hermes](https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/main/agent/image_routing.py).

- **PLANNER-01** — Risk: Untrusted retrieved text can override objective in model reasoning. **Mitigation:** Explicit source/authority separation and tool gating independent of prompt.
- **PLANNER-02** — Risk: Planner can invent unavailable operation or parameter. **Mitigation:** Strict typed registry validation and bounded corrective retries.
- **PLANNER-03** — Risk: Planner can choose an allowed tool with harmful argument. **Mitigation:** Check destination/resource/risk and user intention independently.
- **PLANNER-04** — Risk: Long context can omit original user constraint. **Mitigation:** Durable typed intent, constraint ledger and continuation assertion.
- **PLANNER-05** — Risk: Model can fabricate citations or external facts. **Mitigation:** Source-linked evidence and verifier on important factual outputs.
- **PLANNER-06** — Risk: Deterministic repetitive tasks can waste model calls and tokens. **Mitigation:** Guarded code/native action path and measured batching.
- **PLANNER-07** — Risk: Planner can replan while earlier unacknowledged effect executes. **Mitigation:** Cancellation epochs, leases and effect reconciliation before replan.
- **PLANNER-08** — Risk: Model result can produce unsafe HTML, command or script. **Mitigation:** Contextual escaping, no implicit execution and least-privilege sandbox.

### AGENTS: Parallel agent races, delegation and quality (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/jarvis/planner.server.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/jarvis/planner.server.ts); **reference:** [Ruflo](https://github.com/ruvnet/ruflo/blob/main/plugins/ruflo-browser/README.md).

- **AGENTS-01** — Risk: Two agents can modify same object without serialization. **Mitigation:** Resource-scoped read/write leases and revisioned transactional writes.
- **AGENTS-02** — Risk: Agent message can be forged as coordinator approval. **Mitigation:** Authenticated message envelope and central permission authority.
- **AGENTS-03** — Risk: Agent can outlive cancellation after network partition. **Mitigation:** Expiring lease with fencing epoch at every effect.
- **AGENTS-04** — Risk: Circular task dependency can deadlock worker pool. **Mitigation:** DAG cycle validation and bounded deadlock detection.
- **AGENTS-05** — Risk: A worker can claim success without artifact verification. **Mitigation:** Receipt-backed independent oracle on each milestone.
- **AGENTS-06** — Risk: Coordinator can overdelegate to weak models and reduce correctness. **Mitigation:** Qualification-based assignment, confidence limits and deterministic arbiter.
- **AGENTS-07** — Risk: Shared context or memory can leak from one user task to another. **Mitigation:** Per-tenant context partitioning and redacted event fan-out.
- **AGENTS-08** — Risk: Parallel workers can overwhelm resource budgets and starve foreground user. **Mitigation:** Admission control with CPU/GPU/VRAM quotas and priority inversion safeguards.

### DESKTOP: Native Windows/macOS computer control (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [device-agent/windows/ocade_agent/windows_worker.py](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/device-agent/windows/ocade_agent/windows_worker.py); **reference:** [CUA](https://github.com/trycua/cua/blob/main/docs/content/docs/cua-driver/concepts/how-cua-driver-works.mdx).

- **DESKTOP-01** — Risk: Stale coordinate can click destructive unrelated button. **Mitigation:** Bind coordinate to fresh capture and verify target identity before effect.
- **DESKTOP-02** — Risk: Accessibility element can be recycled after navigation. **Mitigation:** Stable window/process generation and snapshot-bound element reference.
- **DESKTOP-03** — Risk: Background automation can steal physical keyboard focus. **Mitigation:** Native background-safe action path; foreground only with clear consent.
- **DESKTOP-04** — Risk: Protected screen can be captured despite user intent. **Mitigation:** Explicit window capture grants and forbidden-surface detection.
- **DESKTOP-05** — Risk: Visual template matching can mistake repeated matching controls. **Mitigation:** Semantic target + local visual regions + ambiguity refusal.
- **DESKTOP-06** — Risk: Companion worker can execute command with excess OS privilege. **Mitigation:** OS sandbox, privilege dropping and process tree containment.
- **DESKTOP-07** — Risk: Mac/Windows UI difference can invalidate task replay. **Mitigation:** Capability-aware adapter and per-platform qualification.
- **DESKTOP-08** — Risk: Local STOP can fail if model, server or gateway is unavailable. **Mitigation:** Independent offline STOP path, durable fencing and recovery proof.

### BROWSER: Browser, DOM, navigation and web automation (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [device-agent/windows/ocade_agent/web_worker.py](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/device-agent/windows/ocade_agent/web_worker.py); **reference:** [Nod](https://github.com/n8n-io/nod/blob/main/src/providers/agent-browser/index.ts).

- **BROWSER-01** — Risk: Browser action can target a stale or duplicated selector, including a previously learned workflow replayed after an app/site update. **Mitigation:** Check fresh ref/version, unique target and current app/site identity before every mutation; requalify saved procedures and quarantine workflows whose targets or effects have drifted.
- **BROWSER-02** — Risk: Allowed website can redirect to unapproved third-party domain. **Mitigation:** Revalidate final origin and scope on every navigation.
- **BROWSER-03** — Risk: Browser extension can intercept DOM and steal page context. **Mitigation:** Isolated automation browser profile and least-privilege extension policy.
- **BROWSER-04** — Risk: Webpage can inject fake tool result or system instruction. **Mitigation:** Treat page text as untrusted and separate tool authority.
- **BROWSER-05** — Risk: Browser screenshot can include secret or unrelated tab. **Mitigation:** Target-specific capture and redaction before provider transfer.
- **BROWSER-06** — Risk: Cross-origin iframe can trick click targeting. **Mitigation:** Frame-scoped locator and origin-specific permission.
- **BROWSER-07** — Risk: Dynamic app can report optimistic save before persistence. **Mitigation:** Post-action server-side readback of durable saved result.
- **BROWSER-08** — Risk: Browser state can be hijacked through shared cookies/profile. **Mitigation:** Per-user authenticated browser contexts and secure session lifecycle.

### VERIFY: Independent verification, evidence and misleading success (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/jarvis/supervisor.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/jarvis/supervisor.ts); **reference:** [CUA](https://github.com/trycua/cua/blob/main/docs/content/docs/cua-driver/concepts/how-cua-driver-works.mdx).

- **VERIFY-01** — Risk: File-exists check can pass a wrong or empty deliverable. **Mitigation:** Check content, format, semantics and independent open/recompute.
- **VERIFY-02** — Risk: Worker can report success without signed effect evidence. **Mitigation:** Receipt signature and independent postcondition verification.
- **VERIFY-03** — Risk: Validator can test same mistaken assumptions as generator. **Mitigation:** Independent oracle, adversarial fixtures and diversity of checks.
- **VERIFY-04** — Risk: Failed checks can be changed to make task pass. **Mitigation:** Freeze outcome criteria and audit changes with user approval.
- **VERIFY-05** — Risk: Partially completed job can be marked complete by timeout. **Mitigation:** Explicit partial/unknown states and no fake percent.
- **VERIFY-06** — Risk: Evidence artifact can be swapped after verification. **Mitigation:** Hash binding to source, output, verifier version and run ID.
- **VERIFY-07** — Risk: Flaky verifier can be treated as definitive green. **Mitigation:** Repeat on controlled fixture, quantify flakes and require stable proof.
- **VERIFY-08** — Risk: User-visible success can precede underlying transaction commit. **Mitigation:** Final confirmation only after durable state and receipt reconciliation.

### EFFECT: External effects: payment, send, deletion and publish (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/jarvis/authority.server.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/jarvis/authority.server.ts); **reference:** [CUA](https://github.com/trycua/cua/blob/main/docs/content/docs/cua-driver/concepts/how-cua-driver-works.mdx).

- **EFFECT-01** — Risk: Transaction can be repeated after ambiguous timeout. **Mitigation:** External idempotency key, reconcile remote receipt and require safe recovery.
- **EFFECT-02** — Risk: Approval can be reused for a different recipient or amount. **Mitigation:** Bind authorization to exact canonical effect digest.
- **EFFECT-03** — Risk: A low-risk tool can invoke high-risk downstream action. **Mitigation:** Server-owned effect classification and escalating authorization.
- **EFFECT-04** — Risk: Stop may arrive after external service already committed action. **Mitigation:** Show irreversible effect clearly and reconcile rather than claim undo.
- **EFFECT-05** — Risk: Agent can click Publish or Send based only on model confidence. **Mitigation:** Human review for consequential external effects and exact preview.
- **EFFECT-06** — Risk: Deletion can remove shared resource without validating dependencies. **Mitigation:** Dependency map, legal retention and explicit affected-item preview.
- **EFFECT-07** — Risk: Transaction can commit despite depleted budget or revoked rights. **Mitigation:** Revalidate budget and authority atomically immediately before dispatch.
- **EFFECT-08** — Risk: Remote service may accept request twice despite local deduplication. **Mitigation:** Use provider idempotency contract or reconciliation before any retry.

### BILLING: Plans, entitlements, payments and free-tier abuse (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/commercial/generation-gate.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/commercial/generation-gate.ts); **reference:** [Ruflo](https://github.com/ruvnet/ruflo/blob/main/plugins/ruflo-browser/README.md).

- **BILLING-01** — Risk: Client-side plan selection can be forged to unlock paid features. **Mitigation:** Server-owned entitlements from verified payment state.
- **BILLING-02** — Risk: User can bypass free caps by editing local counters. **Mitigation:** Atomic server-side permits and usage windows.
- **BILLING-03** — Risk: Webhook delivery can be duplicated or reordered. **Mitigation:** Signed webhook validation and idempotent payment state transitions.
- **BILLING-04** — Risk: Trial/free promotion can be reopened through account churn. **Mitigation:** Abuse-resistant eligibility with privacy-preserving signals.
- **BILLING-05** — Risk: Offline credits can be replayed on cloned devices. **Mitigation:** Device-bound signed leases, monotonic counters and settlement.
- **BILLING-06** — Risk: Concurrent stream and cancellation can avoid usage settlement. **Mitigation:** Durable permit finalization and reconciliation of charged work.
- **BILLING-07** — Risk: Subscription downgrade may not revoke active expensive jobs. **Mitigation:** Entitlement epoch check at each new costly operation.
- **BILLING-08** — Risk: Refund or chargeback can leave active paid access. **Mitigation:** Verified reversal events, grace policy and bounded revocation.

### OFFLINE: Offline computation, reconnect and data consistency (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/commercial/offline-lease.server.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/commercial/offline-lease.server.ts); **reference:** [CUA](https://github.com/trycua/cua/blob/main/docs/content/docs/cua-driver/concepts/how-cua-driver-works.mdx).

- **OFFLINE-01** — Risk: Offline usage can bypass quota when clocks roll backward. **Mitigation:** Signed monotonic counters and server reconciliation.
- **OFFLINE-02** — Risk: Disconnected task can act after revocation. **Mitigation:** Expiring offline grants and effect scope restricted to local reversible operations.
- **OFFLINE-03** — Risk: Two devices can overwrite divergent offline edits. **Mitigation:** Version vectors or merge conflict resolution preserving both revisions.
- **OFFLINE-04** — Risk: Partial model download can be treated as valid local runtime. **Mitigation:** Manifest digest, complete atomic promotion and integrity checks.
- **OFFLINE-05** — Risk: Local cache can retain secrets after sign-out or deletion. **Mitigation:** Encrypted per-account storage and key revocation/wipe guarantees.
- **OFFLINE-06** — Risk: Offline-first claim can conceal a cloud fallback. **Mitigation:** Explicit network route tracking and no-background-provider policy.
- **OFFLINE-07** — Risk: Power loss can corrupt checkpoint and resume wrong step. **Mitigation:** Write-ahead journal, checksums, crash replay and effect resolution.
- **OFFLINE-08** — Risk: Time skew can break signed lease and action ordering. **Mitigation:** Monotonic sequence, trusted server time at issue and tolerance bounds.

### ADMIN: Staff, admin, support and emergency access (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/routes/_authenticated/admin.tsx](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/routes/_authenticated/admin.tsx); **reference:** [Ruflo](https://github.com/ruvnet/ruflo/blob/main/plugins/ruflo-browser/README.md).

- **ADMIN-01** — Risk: Staff role check can trust unverified client claims. **Mitigation:** Backend role lookup and action-specific authorization.
- **ADMIN-02** — Risk: Support can view customer data without case grant. **Mitigation:** Scoped, expiring reason-coded support access with audit.
- **ADMIN-03** — Risk: Admin privilege can remain after offboarding. **Mitigation:** Immediate revocation and session/worker invalidation.
- **ADMIN-04** — Risk: Operator export can leak secret keys or PII. **Mitigation:** Field-level redaction, export approval and secure retention.
- **ADMIN-05** — Risk: Emergency override can silently bypass normal control. **Mitigation:** Dual control, time-bound break-glass and visible incident trail.
- **ADMIN-06** — Risk: Administrative tool can mutate multiple tenants accidentally. **Mitigation:** Exact tenant-bound preview and two-person review for bulk actions.
- **ADMIN-07** — Risk: Staff impersonation feature can obscure actor provenance. **Mitigation:** Distinct actor and subject identities in every audited action.
- **ADMIN-08** — Risk: Security logs can omit failed or blocked admin operations. **Mitigation:** Append-only audit event coverage and alert on unusual privilege attempts.

### FRONTEND: Frontend, DevTools, XSS and reverse engineering (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/security/csp-nonce.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/security/csp-nonce.ts); **reference:** [Nod](https://github.com/n8n-io/nod/blob/main/src/providers/agent-browser/index.ts).

- **FRONTEND-01** — Risk: Developer Tools can remove a visual paywall. **Mitigation:** Treat browser as hostile; enforce all entitlements server-side.
- **FRONTEND-02** — Risk: Bundled client secret can be extracted from JavaScript. **Mitigation:** Publishable identifiers only; never ship service or provider secrets.
- **FRONTEND-03** — Risk: XSS can read customer content or invoke session actions. **Mitigation:** Contextual encoding, strict CSP and safe rich-content rendering.
- **FRONTEND-04** — Risk: Service worker can cache or leak prior user's sensitive response. **Mitigation:** Versioned private cache separation and explicit clear on sign-out.
- **FRONTEND-05** — Risk: Extension may read tokens, prompts or screenshots in browser. **Mitigation:** Minimize browser-held secrets, restrictive extension profile and warning.
- **FRONTEND-06** — Risk: API can be replayed without genuine page UI. **Mitigation:** Authorize all requests; anti-replay and rate limit server-side.
- **FRONTEND-07** — Risk: Source maps and bundles can reveal proprietary implementation detail. **Mitigation:** Keep sensitive algorithms, keys, policy and quotas on server; limit debug artifacts.
- **FRONTEND-08** — Risk: Obfuscation or hidden button can create false security belief. **Mitigation:** Trust no client-provided plan or role; automated tamper-resistant API tests.

### PRIVACY: Privacy, memory, deletion, backup and retention (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/library/tombstones.server.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/library/tombstones.server.ts); **reference:** [Ruflo](https://github.com/ruvnet/ruflo/blob/main/plugins/ruflo-browser/README.md).

- **PRIVACY-01** — Risk: Account deletion can leave bytes in storage or pending tombstone. **Mitigation:** Durable deletion queue, tombstone retries and eventual purge proof.
- **PRIVACY-02** — Risk: Vector memory can continue serving deleted facts. **Mitigation:** Provenance-bound deletion through vector index and caches.
- **PRIVACY-03** — Risk: Cross-user memory may be mixed by shared summarization task. **Mitigation:** User/tenant binding at retrieval, summary, storage and prompt assembly.
- **PRIVACY-04** — Risk: Retention policy can be contradicted by crash diagnostics. **Mitigation:** Content-free logs, bounded TTL and retention enforcement.
- **PRIVACY-05** — Risk: Private screenshots can be uploaded without informed consent. **Mitigation:** Explicit provider/region/retention consent and default local processing.
- **PRIVACY-06** — Risk: Share link can outlive intended purpose. **Mitigation:** Scope, expiration and revocation on each read.
- **PRIVACY-07** — Risk: User export may omit hidden personal tables or copies. **Mitigation:** Data-inventory coverage test and service-accessible export lineage.
- **PRIVACY-08** — Risk: Backups may retain erased data beyond stated deletion window. **Mitigation:** Document backup limits, crypto-erasure policy and restore-time deletion replay.

### OPS: CI, deployment, migration and incident operations (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [.github/workflows/ci.yml](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/.github/workflows/ci.yml); **reference:** [Ruflo](https://github.com/ruvnet/ruflo/blob/main/plugins/ruflo-browser/README.md).

- **OPS-01** — Risk: Green CI on earlier SHA can be misreported as production readiness. **Mitigation:** Artifact-to-SHA attestations and same-SHA mandatory gate.
- **OPS-02** — Risk: Production database can lack a migration the code assumes exists. **Mitigation:** Deployment preflight schema version check and backward-compatible rollout.
- **OPS-03** — Risk: Canary can expose a new auth bug to every tenant. **Mitigation:** Progressive rollout with kill switch and isolation regression.
- **OPS-04** — Risk: Rollback can revive revoked grants or stale schema privileges. **Mitigation:** Forward-compatible rollback and explicit authority consistency test.
- **OPS-05** — Risk: CI may skip unsupported platforms but report overall success. **Mitigation:** Required platform matrix and honest unsupported status.
- **OPS-06** — Risk: Security alerting may miss silent data exfiltration. **Mitigation:** Egress/tenant anomaly alerts with independently retained audit.
- **OPS-07** — Risk: A compromised deploy token can publish malicious update. **Mitigation:** Short-lived deploy identity, approvals and signed artifacts.
- **OPS-08** — Risk: An outage can trap tasks with partial external effects. **Mitigation:** Durable incident mode, pause admission and deterministic reconciliation.

### RELIABILITY: Abuse resistance, performance and user experience (8 roots; 48 negative tests)

**Source anchor:** [src/lib/intelligence/edge-handler.ts](https://github.com/BREVEX-CEO/caera-connect-plan/blob/ocade/p00-p22-implementation/src/lib/intelligence/edge-handler.ts); **reference:** [OpenClaw](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/main/docs/tools/browser/configuration.md).

- **RELIABILITY-01** — Risk: Tiny repeated requests can exhaust database and gateway capacity. **Mitigation:** Multi-dimensional quotas, admission control and rate alert.
- **RELIABILITY-02** — Risk: Large attachments can exhaust memory before rejection. **Mitigation:** Streaming size checks and backpressure before base64 expansion.
- **RELIABILITY-03** — Risk: Long-running retries can continue forever after provider outage. **Mitigation:** Finite state machine with retry budget and exposed blocker.
- **RELIABILITY-04** — Risk: Users can confuse working progress animation with verified work. **Mitigation:** Receipt-based progress and explicit unknown/blocked states.
- **RELIABILITY-05** — Risk: Concurrent renders can make foreground apps unusable. **Mitigation:** Resource governor and background job scheduling.
- **RELIABILITY-06** — Risk: Error message may mislead about what was saved or charged. **Mitigation:** Separate model, execution, receipt and billing statuses.
- **RELIABILITY-07** — Risk: Accessibility regression may hide Stop or consent controls. **Mitigation:** WCAG tests, keyboard traversal and focus safety on every form factor.
- **RELIABILITY-08** — Risk: Lack of incident recovery process can prolong harm. **Mitigation:** Tested backups, restore drills, runbooks, on-call and customer notice controls.

## Negative test design register: 1,152 scenario-specific cases

The detailed register below expands each of the 192 root risks against six **domain-specific** adversarial fixtures. Each test is a proposed staging negative test, not a detected vulnerability or a test result. For sortable tabular use download `OCADE_1152_negative_test_scenarios.csv`.

### AUTH — Authentication and account recovery

**AUTH-01. Password recovery link can be reused after successful recovery**

- **OT-01-01-01** — For password recovery link can be reused after successful recovery, login with an identifier belonging to a second account. **Pass condition:** Deny or safely contain the negative condition; enforce: single-use token redemption, rotation of all sessions and replay-negative test; no unauthorized external effect, tenant disclosure, hidden model charge, or false completion.
- **OT-01-01-02** — For password recovery link can be reused after successful recovery, perform operation after password reset and old token survival. **Pass condition:** Deny or safely contain the negative condition; enforce: single-use token redemption, rotation of all sessions and replay-negative test; no unauthorized external effect, tenant disclosure, hidden model charge, or false completion.
- **OT-01-01-03** — For password recovery link can be reused after successful recovery, submit two conflicting authentication requests simultaneously. **Pass condition:** Deny or safely contain the negative condition; enforce: single-use token redemption, rotation of all sessions and replay-negative test; no unauthorized external effect, tenant disclosure, hidden model charge, or false completion.
- **OT-01-01-04** — For password recovery link can be reused after successful recovery, replay a captured authorization response after session rotation. **Pass condition:** Deny or safely contain the negative condition; enforce: single-use token redemption, rotation of all sessions and replay-negative test; no unauthorized external effect, tenant disclosure, hidden model charge, or false completion.
- **OT-01-01-05** — For password recovery link can be reused after successful recovery, force abrupt network disconnect between proof and session issue. **Pass condition:** Deny or safely contain the negative condition; enforce: single-use token redemption, rotation of all sessions and replay-negative test; no unauthorized external effect, tenant disclosure, hidden model charge, or false completion.
- **OT-01-01-06** — For password recovery link can be reused after successful recovery, use attacker-controlled browser content or extension during return redirect. **Pass condition:** Deny or safely contain the negative condition; enforce: single-use token redemption, rotation of all sessions and replay-negative test; no unauthorized external effect, tenant disclosure, hidden model charge, or false completion.

**AUTH-02. Login rate limiting can be bypassed across distributed identifiers**

- **OT-01-02-01** — For login rate limiting can be bypassed across distributed identifiers, login with an identifier belonging to a second account. **Pass condition:** Deny or safely contain the negative condition; enforce: server-side subject/IP/device risk budget with privacy-safe throttling; no unauthorized external effect, tenant disclosure, hidden model charge, or false completion.
- **OT-01-02-02** — For login rate limiting can be bypassed across distributed identifiers, perform operation after password reset and old token survival. **Pass condition:** Deny or safely contain the negative condition; enforce: server-side subject/IP/device risk budget with privacy-safe throttling; no unauthorized external effect, tenant disclosure, hidden model charge, or false completion.
- **OT-01-02-03** — For login rate limiting can be bypassed across distributed identifiers, submit two conflicting authentication requests simultaneously. **Pass condition:** Deny or safely contain the negative condition; enforce: server-side subject/IP/device risk budget with privacy-safe throttling; no unauthorized external effect, tenant disclosure, hidden model charge, or false completion.
- **OT-01-02-04** — For login rate limiting can be bypassed across distributed identifiers, replay a captured authorization response after session rotation. **Pass condition:** Deny or safely contain the negative condition; enforce: server-side subject/IP/device risk budget with privacy-safe throttling; no unauthorized external effect, tenant disclosure, hidden model charge, or false completion.

## Verification and limits

All tests are NOT RUN. No production exploit has been established. Static source paths are anchors, not proof of vulnerability. Prior green workflows cannot be extrapolated to the current SHA. Real staging proof requires exact SHA, deployed schema version, test identities, synthetic fixture hashes, actual request/response and immutable receipts, absence of leaks, and independent reviewer verdict. The recommended system is meant to be a design improvement over the combined references, **not** a measured performance claim.
